# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Next Steps Plan v0.11 (Single-Host First + Default Model Baseline)

**Date:** 2026-09-13  
**Target executing agent:** GLM 5.3 Flash  
**Environment:** MARION-IA-USA  
**Control plane:** MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager on `10.0.20.108`  
**Status basis:** LLM Manager v0.8.0 hosted-command work is complete; this plan replaces the previous RDMA-focused next-step direction.

---

## 0. Executive objective

Finish the LLM Manager as the stable model-serving control plane for local AI agents.

The immediate operating model is:

```text
Project Implementation Manager              [future]
        |
        v
AI Agent Deployment Service                 [future]
        |
        | create/revoke agent API keys
        | query available models
        v
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager                   [current project]
        |
        | OpenAI-compatible API
        | model selected in each request
        v
+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
| MIAM-00111     | MIAM-00112     | MIAM-00143     | MIAM-00144     | MIAM-00149     |
| VM103          | VM401          | VM109          | VM111          | VM149          |
| GPU inference  | GPU inference  | GPU inference  | GPU inference  | llama.cpp CPU  |
+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
```

The first production model should remain simple:

```text
MIAM-00111 / VM103
    -> QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
    -> served model: qwen3.6-35b-a3b
```

Other machines may host different models independently.

**RDMA / multi-node inference is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for this plan.**
Do not modify, extend, test, or build RDMA workflows while executing v0.11.
Any future RDMA/SR-IOV work will be handled in a separate project and plan.

---

# 1. Non-negotiable execution rules

1. Back up files and database state before schema/application mutations.
2. Start with read-only verification.
3. Never print secrets, passwords, provider master keys, or complete API keys.
4. Preserve LLDAP admin authentication.
5. Preserve audit logging for every administrative mutation.
6. Do not create an arbitrary root shell in the web UI.
7. Model/unit edits must be structured and validated.
8. Saving a hosting configuration must **not** restart a model.
9. Restart/start actions require a separate explicit confirmation.
10. Do not automatically power on a machine that the manager has marked intentionally offline for energy savings.
11. Do automatically recover a machine that is expected to be online but unexpectedly stops.
12. Do not silently route a request to a different model than the `model` value supplied by the client.
13. Do not invent local inference costs. Unknown or incomplete cost data must be shown as unknown/incomplete.
14. Keep MIAM-00112 outside all RDMA logic. RDMA logic is not part of this plan at all.
15. Preserve the existing hosted-command preset/history functionality unless a regression is found.

---

# 2. Verified/current architecture to preserve

Treat these as the current intended components and verify them before changing anything:

```text
LLM Manager UI/API
    10.0.20.108
    current deployed branch/version: v0.8.0-hosted-cmds or later

PostgreSQL
    CT115
    10.0.20.116

Inference backends

MIAM-00111 / VM103
    10.0.20.161:8000
    primary initial production model:
    qwen3.6-35b-a3b

MIAM-00112 / VM401
    10.0.20.162:8000
    Jordan manually started VM401 after it was found stopped
    NOT part of RDMA

MIAM-00143 / VM109
    10.0.20.163:8000

MIAM-00144 / VM111
    10.0.20.164:8000

MIAM-00149 / VM149
    10.0.20.165:8000
    llama.cpp CPU backend
    logical model currently includes startupteams/llamacpp
```

Hosted-command presets/history were already implemented and should remain available for individual hosts.

---

# 3. Phase A — Read-only preflight and recovery audit

## A1. Back up before changes

Create timestamped backups of:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/app/
/etc/llm-manager/
LiteLLM configuration
systemd unit(s)
PostgreSQL schema/data relevant to:
    deployments
    models
    API keys
    hosting command presets
    power/cost data
    audit events
```

Record backup locations in the final report.

## A2. Verify manager services

Confirm:

```text
LLM Manager web service
LiteLLM
PostgreSQL connectivity
nginx/reverse proxy
LLDAP authentication
telemetry collectors
```

## A3. Verify every inference host

For `.161`, `.162`, `.163`, `.164`, `.165`:

1. Determine whether the VM is running.
2. Determine whether it is **supposed** to be running.
3. Check service status.
4. Check TCP/API reachability.
5. Query `/v1/models`.
6. Run one minimal completion if the deployment is supposed to be serving.
7. Record model name, engine, model context limit, service unit, and current health.

### Special handling for MIAM-00112 / VM401

Jordan has already started VM401.

The first task is to verify:

```text
VM401 is running
10.0.20.162 is reachable
SSH host identity is correct
vLLM service is running
/v1/models returns 200
one completion succeeds
```

If the manager has a stale SSH host key for `.162`, reconcile the actual host key safely.

**Do not disable SSH host-key checking globally.**

---

# 4. Phase B — Introduce explicit desired-state / power policy

The manager must distinguish:

```text
unexpected outage
```

from:

```text
intentional power saving
```

This is required before automatic VM restart is enabled.

## B1. Add desired-state fields

Add or confirm a persistent per-managed-deployment/per-host state such as:

```text
desired_power_state
    RUNNING
    STOPPED_INTENTIONAL

management_mode
    MANAGED
    UNMANAGED

desired_service_state
    SERVING
    STOPPED
    MAINTENANCE
```

Exact schema naming may differ, but semantics must be explicit.

## B2. Recovery rules

### If:

```text
management_mode = MANAGED
desired_power_state = RUNNING
```

and the VM is unexpectedly stopped:

```text
1. attempt VM start
2. wait for guest agent / network
3. verify service
4. if service is down, perform bounded service recovery
5. verify /v1/models
6. run health completion
7. restore routing only after health passes
```

### If:

```text
desired_power_state = STOPPED_INTENTIONAL
```

then:

```text
do not power on
do not restart the VM
do not route new traffic to it
do not count it as an outage
display "intentionally offline / energy saving"
```

## B3. Required UI controls

For each managed host/deployment provide a clear operator control:

```text
Desired state:
    Online / managed
    Intentionally offline

Current state:
    Running
    Starting
    Loading model
    Healthy
    Failed
    Intentionally off
```

Changing desired state is an audited admin mutation.

## B4. Bounded recovery

Never create an infinite restart loop.

Suggested default:

```text
VM start attempts:       2
service restart attempts: 2
cooldown:                configurable
then:
    mark FAILED
    remove from routing
    alert operator
```

---

# 5. Phase C — Make the model registry the routing source of truth

The user-facing abstraction is the **model**, not the physical server.

A client API key should be able to use any actively hosted model by supplying the standard OpenAI `model` field.

## C1. Logical model registry

Create or normalize a registry containing at least:

```text
logical_model_name
display_name
engine
deployment_id
backend_url
host
VM
health
desired_state
routable
context_limit
concurrency_limit
capabilities
last_verified
```

Example:

```text
logical model:
    qwen3.6-35b-a3b

deployment:
    MIAM-00111 / VM103

backend:
    http://10.0.20.161:8000/v1
```

A model may later move to another host without forcing client key changes.

## C2. Routability gate

A model appears routable only when:

```text
VM desired state = RUNNING
VM current state = running
model service healthy
/v1/models succeeds
health completion succeeds
deployment not drained
deployment not in maintenance
```

## C3. `GET /v1/models`

For a normal LLM Manager agent key:

```text
GET /v1/models
```

must return the models currently available to that key.

Default policy for normal internal agent keys:

```text
allow all actively hosted/routable models
```

Do not invent a default model.

If a model is offline or intentionally powered down, either:

```text
omit it from /v1/models
```

or clearly mark it unavailable if the compatibility layer supports doing so without violating client expectations.

Prefer omission for standard OpenAI-compatible behavior.

---

# 6. Phase D — Standard OpenAI-compatible API behavior

The LLM Manager should behave like one stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint.

Clients should use:

```text
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
```

and send:

```json
{
  "model": "qwen3.6-35b-a3b",
  "messages": [...]
}
```

## D1. No default model

Do not assign a default model to an API key.

If a request omits `model`:

```text
return a normal explicit request validation error
```

Do not silently pick a model.

## D2. No deployment pinning

Do not add custom client-visible semantics such as:

```text
pin this API key to MIAM-00111
pin this key to deployment 123
```

unless a future OpenAI-compatible mechanism naturally supports it.

Routing implementation details remain server-side.

## D3. Required compatibility endpoints

Maintain at minimum:

```text
GET  /v1/models
POST /v1/chat/completions
```

Where already supported, preserve:

```text
POST /v1/responses
POST /v1/embeddings
```

but do not delay the core work to add unused endpoints.

## D4. Exact model routing

When a request specifies:

```text
model = X
```

the manager must:

```text
1. resolve X in the logical model registry
2. confirm an eligible deployment exists
3. route to that deployment
4. record which physical deployment handled it
5. return an explicit model-unavailable error if X is not currently hosted
```

Do not substitute a different model merely because it is healthy.

---

# 7. Phase E — API keys become generic agent credentials

API keys authenticate the agent.

They do **not** encode a server endpoint and do **not** assign a default model.

## E1. Default API-key behavior

A normal internal AI-agent key should permit:

```text
all actively hosted local models
```

selected through the standard request `model` field.

Keep the ability to add restrictions later, but do not make model whitelisting mandatory for normal agent deployment.

## E2. Preserve one key per agent

Each semi-persistent agent should receive its own key.

Store metadata such as:

```text
key_id
alias
project_id
agent_instance_id
agent_name
harness
created_at
last_used_at
enabled
revoked_at
monthly budget
request/token usage
```

Only show the raw key once at creation/rotation.

## E3. API-key administration UI

For each key display:

```text
alias
agent/project association
status
created
last used
requests
tokens
provider spend
estimated local compute cost
monthly cap if configured
```

Actions:

```text
block
unblock
rotate
revoke
```

Do not show a misleading "endpoint" field.

Instead show:

```text
API base:
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1

Model access:
All currently hosted models
```

---

# 8. Phase F — Make model hosting easy from the web UI

The UI should let an operator easily change what each machine is hosting.

## F1. Keep the existing Edit unit/model workflow

Each deployment card should retain:

```text
Edit unit / model
Restart model
Logs
```

and the already-built hosting-command preset/history system.

## F2. Improve the Edit unit/model modal

Show:

```text
Current active configuration
Saved hosting presets
Preset name
Engine
Model/repo
Served model name
Context limit
TP
DP
EP
quantization
KV-cache configuration
memory utilization
command/systemd unit preview
last used
use count
```

Actions:

```text
Load preset
Rename
Delete
Drag reorder
Save configuration
```

## F3. Save does not restart

The workflow must remain:

```text
Edit
-> Save
-> validate
-> show "saved; not yet applied"
```

Then the operator separately clicks:

```text
Restart model
```

The UI must show a confirmation such as:

```text
Restart qwen3.6-35b-a3b on MIAM-00111 / VM103 now?
Active requests may be interrupted.
```

Only after confirmation:

```text
drain if possible
restart service
wait for health
run verification completion
restore routability
```

## F4. Hosting a different model

When changing the served model:

```text
1. save new preset/config
2. validate paths/arguments
3. preserve old preset
4. require explicit restart
5. detect new model from /v1/models
6. update logical model registry
7. mark previous model unavailable unless another healthy deployment serves it
8. refresh LiteLLM routing
9. verify through the public LLM Manager endpoint
```

No direct manual LiteLLM YAML editing should be required for routine model changes after this phase.

---

# 9. Phase G — Initial production topology

Do not attempt multi-node serving in this plan.

## G1. Initial primary model

Keep:

```text
MIAM-00111 / VM103
QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
served name: qwen3.6-35b-a3b
```

as the first primary production deployment.

Verify through the manager:

```text
GET /v1/models
POST /v1/chat/completions
```

using a normal per-agent key.

## G2. Other hosts

MIAM-00112, MIAM-00143, MIAM-00144, and MIAM-00149 may host different models independently.

The manager should not assume that all GPU servers host the same model.

Example future state:

```text
MIAM-00111 -> qwen3.6-35b-a3b
MIAM-00112 -> another smaller/fast model
MIAM-00143 -> another specialist model
MIAM-00144 -> another model
MIAM-00149 -> startupteams/llamacpp
```

All should appear through the same `/v1/models` endpoint.

---

# 10. Phase H — Correct the cost model

The current interface must stop presenting `$0.0000` as though local inference has no cost.

There are multiple different concepts and they must be separated.

## H1. Cost categories

Display at least:

### Provider spend

```text
Actual provider spend
```

For local models:

```text
$0 provider spend
```

is legitimate.

But this is **not** total inference cost.

### Direct compute energy

Primary local metric.

Estimate direct compute power from:

```text
measured GPU watts
+
CPU/platform/RAM overhead estimate
```

Use real GPU telemetry whenever possible.

### Direct compute electricity cost

Primary local cost metric:

```text
direct_compute_kWh * current_electricity_rate
```

### Facility operational energy/cost

Secondary metric:

```text
Emporia whole-compute circuits
cooling
other facility loads
```

Keep cooling separate from the main direct-compute metric.

## H2. Required labels

Never show a calculated value without explaining what it is.

Use labels such as:

```text
Provider spend: ACTUAL
GPU energy: MEASURED
Host overhead: ESTIMATED
Direct compute cost: ESTIMATED
Facility energy: MEASURED (Emporia)
Cooling allocation: MEASURED/ESTIMATED as appropriate
```

## H3. Never fabricate zero

If telemetry is absent:

```text
—
Insufficient telemetry
Coverage incomplete
```

Do not substitute:

```text
$0.0000
```

unless the quantity being displayed is truly known to be zero.

## H4. Telemetry coverage

Every time-based cost/energy total must include telemetry coverage.

Example:

```text
Last 24h direct compute:
5.82 kWh
$0.92 estimated
telemetry coverage: 99.3%
```

If only 60 minutes of samples exist, do not label that result "24h".

## H5. Sanity test the energy integrator

Implement automated tests such as:

```text
100 watts for exactly 1 hour
= 0.100 kWh
```

and:

```text
1000 watts for exactly 1 hour
= 1.000 kWh
```

Fail deployment if the integrator does not pass.

## H6. Rate model

Keep the configured Marion/Alliant seasonal rate components versioned.

Operational planning totals currently include:

```text
winter base energy rate
summer base energy rate
year-round per-kWh fee/rider component
```

Do not collapse the components into an unexplained constant.

## H7. Request-level local cost

When enough telemetry exists, estimate per-request energy cost.

Store:

```text
request_id
model
deployment
start/end
input tokens
output tokens
TTFT
latency
average/attributed watts
estimated Wh
estimated $
confidence
```

If multiple concurrent requests overlap, explicitly use an allocation method and mark the result as estimated.

Do not imply request-level energy is utility-meter exact.

---

# 11. Phase I — Redesign Spend & Efficiency UI

The existing table should be revised so `$0.0000` cannot be misread.

Recommended columns:

```text
Model
Requests
Tokens
Provider spend
Direct compute energy
Estimated direct compute cost
Avg TTFT
Output tok/s
Cost / 1M tokens
Energy / 1M tokens
Telemetry coverage
```

For local models:

```text
Provider spend = $0.00 actual
Estimated direct compute cost = non-zero when telemetry exists
```

For OpenRouter:

```text
Provider spend = provider-reported actual
Direct local compute = n/a
```

Add help text:

```text
Provider spend and local compute cost are separate metrics.
Local model provider spend may be $0 while electricity cost is non-zero.
```

---

# 12. Phase J — Foundation for Project Implementation Manager / AI Agent Deployment

Do not build the Project Implementation Manager in this phase.

However, the LLM Manager API must be designed so the future system can use it cleanly.

Future architecture:

```text
Project Implementation Manager
        |
        v
AI Agent Deployment Service
        |
        +-- instantiate VM/LXC/container/process
        +-- select harness
        +-- create semi-persistent agent identity
        +-- request LLM Manager API key
        +-- inject:
              OPENAI_BASE_URL
              OPENAI_API_KEY
        |
        v
Agent chooses model per normal OpenAI request
```

## J1. Add machine-to-machine key lifecycle API

Provide authenticated administrative endpoints suitable for the future deployment service:

```text
create agent key
rotate agent key
revoke agent key
disable/enable agent key
query key metadata
query key usage
```

Use an LLDAP/service identity or other strong internal service authentication.

Do not expose the LiteLLM master key to the future deployment service.

## J2. Idempotent provisioning

The future service should be able to send an external identity such as:

```text
project_id
agent_instance_id
```

If provisioning is retried, the LLM Manager should not create duplicate agents/keys accidentally.

Use an idempotency key or stable external ID.

## J3. Return only what the deployment service needs

On creation return:

```text
agent/key ID
raw API key once
OpenAI base URL
metadata
```

Do **not** return:

```text
physical backend IP
provider master credentials
server SSH details
```

## J4. Agent model discovery

The deployed agent can call:

```text
GET /v1/models
```

with its own API key.

It then selects the model using the standard OpenAI request body.

This keeps the future deployment service decoupled from physical inference topology.

---

# 13. Phase K — Health-aware model availability

A model must disappear from the usable routing pool if its deployment is unhealthy.

## K1. Health loop

For each desired-online managed deployment collect:

```text
VM status
guest reachability
service status
/v1/models
small completion
GPU health where applicable
```

## K2. Failure transition

If unhealthy:

```text
remove from routing first
attempt bounded recovery
retest
restore only when healthy
```

{"success": true, "diff": "--- a//home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py\n+++ b//home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py\n@@ -58,12 +58,25 @@\n \n \n def guest_exec(node, vmid, command, timeout=120):\n-    \"\"\"Run command in guest via qemu-guest-agent, wait for completion, return (stdout, stderr, exitcode).\"\"\"\n-    r = api('POST', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec',\n-            {'command': json.dumps(command), 'timeout': timeout})\n-    pid = r['data']\n+    \"\"\"Run command in guest via qemu-guest-agent, wait for completion, return (stdout, stderr, exitcode).\n+    PVE expects 'command' as repeated form fields (arg encode_array).\"\"\"\n+    # Build the raw body manually: command[]=cmd1&command[]=cmd2 ...\n+    # Use arg= prefixed keys per PVE API convention for arrays.\n+    parts = []\n+    for c in command:\n+        parts.append('command[]=' + urllib.parse.quote(c, safe=''))\n+    # also encode vga + timeout\n+    body = '&'.join(parts).encode()\n+    url = PVE + f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec?timeout={timeout}'\n+    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n+    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}', 'CSRFPreventionToken': csrf,\n+               'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded'}\n+    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=body, headers=headers, method='POST')\n+    resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=timeout + 30)\n+    pid = json.load(resp)['data']\n     import time\n-    for _ in range(timeout):\n+    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n+    while time.time() < deadline:\n         time.sleep(2)\n         try:\n             st = api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec-status', {'pid': pid})['data']\n", "files_modified": ["/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py"], "lint": {"status": "ok", "output": ""}, "resolved_path": "/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py"}

{"success": true, "diff": "--- a//home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py\n+++ b//home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py\n@@ -31,7 +31,12 @@\n     headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}'}\n     data = None\n     if body is not None:\n-        data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body).encode()\n+        enc = urllib.parse.urlencode(body).encode()\n+        if method == 'GET':\n+            # GET params must go in the querystring for PVE\n+            path = path + ('&' if '?' in path else '?') + enc.decode()\n+        else:\n+            data = enc\n     if method != 'GET':\n         headers['CSRFPreventionToken'] = csrf\n     req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + path, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)\n", "files_modified": ["/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py"], "lint": {"status": "ok", "output": ""}, "resolved_path": "/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/pve.py"}

{"output": "def ldap_login(username, password):\n    \"\"\"Return (role|None, error|None). Binds as service account, finds user,\n    verifies password by binding as the user, maps groups to a role.\"\"\"\n    if not LDAP_BIND_PW:\n        return None, \"server: bind credentials missing\"\n    try:\n        srv = ldap3.Server(LDAP_HOST, port=LDAP_PORT, get_info=None)\n        conn = ldap3.Connection(srv, user=LDAP_BIND_DN, password=LDAP_BIND_PW,\n                                auto_bind=True, raise_exceptions=True)\n    except Exception as e:\n        return None, f\"server: service bind failed: {str(e)[:80]}\"\n    try:\n        conn.search(LDAP_BASE, f\"(uid={ldap3.utils.conv.escape_filter_chars(username)})\",\n                    attributes=[\"displayName\"])\n        if not conn.entries:\n            return None, \"unknown user\"\n        user_dn = conn.entries[0].entry_dn\n    finally:\n        conn.unbind()\n    # map groups: search ou=groups for entries whose member=<user_dn> (LLDAP has no memberOf)\n    groups = []\n    try:\n        gconn = ldap3.Connection(srv, user=LDAP_BIND_DN, password=LDAP_BIND_PW,\n                                 auto_bind=True, raise_exceptions=True)\n        gconn.search(f\"ou=groups,{LDAP_BASE}\",\n                     f\"(member={ldap3.utils.conv.escape_filter_chars(user_dn)})\",\n                     attributes=[\"cn\"])\n        groups = [e.cn.value for e in gconn.entries]\n        gconn.unbind()\n    except Exception:\n        groups = []\n    try:\n        uconn = ldap3.Connection(srv, user=user_dn, password=password,\n                                 auto_bind=True, raise_exceptions=True)\n        uconn.unbind()\n    except Exception:\n        return None, \"invalid credentials\"\n    role = None\n    for g in groups:\n        m = re.match(rf\"{re.escape(GROUP_PREFIX)}(\\S+)\", g or \"\")", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

| **K — Health gating** | Unhealthy host → `routable=FALSE` → omitted from gated `/v1/models`; intentional-off is never "repaired" | Gated `/v1/models` returns 401 without key (nginx exact-match route added) |

## Session Progress Report — v0.11 LLM Manager + Phase 19 Benchmarks

### ✅ Completed this session

**1. Phase L acceptance tests — ALL PASS**
- **Desired-state E2E (VM111/.164)**: Set `STOPPED_INTENTIONAL` → recovery engine logged `intentional_state_pending` and made **zero recovery attempts** across multiple ticks; `/power-off` correctly gated (409 unless intentional-off); VM stayed stopped ~4 min. Restored to `RUNNING` → engine detected `outage_detected` → `recovery_vm_start` → `recovery_success` → backend healthy → registry `routable=t`. Full state machine verified live.
- **Agent-key E2E**: Issued `v011-e2e-testkey`; gated `/v1/models` returns 200 with agent key; chat completions route correctly through the manager.
- **Routing attribution FIXED**: Discovered LiteLLM v1.100 dispatches `async_log_success_event` for async proxy requests — my sync-only callback was registered but never invoked. Added an async wrapper (executor delegation); `request_routing_log` now records model + resolved `api_base` + tokens + latency per request (§D4.4 verified with real rows).
- **Service API idempotency**: `provision` ×2 with same external ID → `status: existing`, same key_id, same raw key. §J2 pass.

**2. Four §16 reports written** (local + to be archived on VM114):
- `LLM-MANAGER-v0.11-COMPLETION-REPORT.md` — full 14-section completion report
- `LLM-MANAGER-v0.11-REGRESSION-RESULTS.md` — 24/24 regression tests pass, 2 rollout issues documented+fixed
- `LLM-MANAGER-v0.11-API-MODEL-ROUTING.md` — routing contract, gate logic, alias status
- `LLM-MANAGER-v0.11-COST-VALIDATION.md` — §H5 gates PASS, metric separation verified

**3. Phase 19 benchmarks — quick matrix COMPLETE (8/8 runs)** on MIAM-00112 (VM401, TP1/DP6/EP6 baseline):

**4. Live incident (system working as designed, documented)**: I accidentally launched two bench jobs concurrently (25K nohup + 32K foreground). The saturation failed the manager's health probe → **recovery engine correctly performed bounded service restart** (§K2: unhealthy → restart → recover). vLLM reloaded cleanly. Lesson recorded: bench legs must run serially.

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Next Steps Plan v0.11 (Single-Host First + Default Model Baseline)

**Date:** 2026-09-13  
**Target executing agent:** GLM 5.3 Flash  
**Environment:** MARION-IA-USA  
**Control plane:** MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager on `10.0.20.108`  
**Status basis:** LLM Manager v0.8.0 hosted-command work is complete; this plan replaces the previous RDMA-focused next-step direction.

---

## 0. Executive objective

Finish the LLM Manager as the stable model-serving control plane for local AI agents.

The immediate operating model is:

```text
Project Implementation Manager              [future]
        |
        v
AI Agent Deployment Service                 [future]
        |
        | create/revoke agent API keys
        | query available models
        v
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager                   [current project]
        |
        | OpenAI-compatible API
        | model selected in each request
        v
+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
| MIAM-00111     | MIAM-00112     | MIAM-00143     | MIAM-00144     | MIAM-00149     |
| VM103          | VM401          | VM109          | VM111          | VM149          |
| GPU inference  | GPU inference  | GPU inference  | GPU inference  | llama.cpp CPU  |
+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
```

The first production model should remain simple:

```text
MIAM-00111 / VM103
    -> QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
    -> served model: qwen3.6-35b-a3b
```

Other machines may host different models independently.

**RDMA / multi-node inference is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for this plan.**
Do not modify, extend, test, or build RDMA workflows while executing v0.11.
Any future RDMA/SR-IOV work will be handled in a separate project and plan.

---

# 1. Non-negotiable execution rules

1. Back up files and database state before schema/application mutations.
2. Start with read-only verification.
3. Never print secrets, passwords, provider master keys, or complete API keys.
4. Preserve LLDAP admin authentication.
5. Preserve audit logging for every administrative mutation.
6. Do not create an arbitrary root shell in the web UI.
7. Model/unit edits must be structured and validated.
8. Saving a hosting configuration must **not** restart a model.
9. Restart/start actions require a separate explicit confirmation.
10. Do not automatically power on a machine that the manager has marked intentionally offline for energy savings.
11. Do automatically recover a machine that is expected to be online but unexpectedly stops.
12. Do not silently route a request to a different model than the `model` value supplied by the client.
13. Do not invent local inference costs. Unknown or incomplete cost data must be shown as unknown/incomplete.
14. Keep MIAM-00112 outside all RDMA logic. RDMA logic is not part of this plan at all.
15. Preserve the existing hosted-command preset/history functionality unless a regression is found.

---

# 2. Verified/current architecture to preserve

Treat these as the current intended components and verify them before changing anything:

```text
LLM Manager UI/API
    10.0.20.108
    current deployed branch/version: v0.8.0-hosted-cmds or later

PostgreSQL
    CT115
    10.0.20.116

Inference backends

MIAM-00111 / VM103
    10.0.20.161:8000
    primary initial production model:
    qwen3.6-35b-a3b

MIAM-00112 / VM401
    10.0.20.162:8000
    Jordan manually started VM401 after it was found stopped
    NOT part of RDMA

MIAM-00143 / VM109
    10.0.20.163:8000

MIAM-00144 / VM111
    10.0.20.164:8000

MIAM-00149 / VM149
    10.0.20.165:8000
    llama.cpp CPU backend
    logical model currently includes startupteams/llamacpp
```

Hosted-command presets/history were already implemented and should remain available for individual hosts.

---

# 3. Phase A — Read-only preflight and recovery audit

## A1. Back up before changes

Create timestamped backups of:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/app/
/etc/llm-manager/
LiteLLM configuration
systemd unit(s)
PostgreSQL schema/data relevant to:
    deployments
    models
    API keys
    hosting command presets
    power/cost data
    audit events
```

Record backup locations in the final report.

## A2. Verify manager services

Confirm:

```text
LLM Manager web service
LiteLLM
PostgreSQL connectivity
nginx/reverse proxy
LLDAP authentication
telemetry collectors
```

## A3. Verify every inference host

For `.161`, `.162`, `.163`, `.164`, `.165`:

1. Determine whether the VM is running.
2. Determine whether it is **supposed** to be running.
3. Check service status.
4. Check TCP/API reachability.
5. Query `/v1/models`.
6. Run one minimal completion if the deployment is supposed to be serving.
7. Record model name, engine, model context limit, service unit, and current health.

### Special handling for MIAM-00112 / VM401

Jordan has already started VM401.

The first task is to verify:

```text
VM401 is running
10.0.20.162 is reachable
SSH host identity is correct
vLLM service is running
/v1/models returns 200
one completion succeeds
```

If the manager has a stale SSH host key for `.162`, reconcile the actual host key safely.

**Do not disable SSH host-key checking globally.**

---

# 4. Phase B — Introduce explicit desired-state / power policy

The manager must distinguish:

```text
unexpected outage
```

from:

```text
intentional power saving
```

This is required before automatic VM restart is enabled.

## B1. Add desired-state fields

Add or confirm a persistent per-managed-deployment/per-host state such as:

```text
desired_power_state
    RUNNING
    STOPPED_INTENTIONAL

management_mode
    MANAGED
    UNMANAGED

desired_service_state
    SERVING
    STOPPED
    MAINTENANCE
```

Exact schema naming may differ, but semantics must be explicit.

## B2. Recovery rules

### If:

```text
management_mode = MANAGED
desired_power_state = RUNNING
```

and the VM is unexpectedly stopped:

```text
1. attempt VM start
2. wait for guest agent / network
3. verify service
4. if service is down, perform bounded service recovery
5. verify /v1/models
6. run health completion
7. restore routing only after health passes
```

### If:

```text
desired_power_state = STOPPED_INTENTIONAL
```

then:

```text
do not power on
do not restart the VM
do not route new traffic to it
do not count it as an outage
display "intentionally offline / energy saving"
```

## B3. Required UI controls

For each managed host/deployment provide a clear operator control:

```text
Desired state:
    Online / managed
    Intentionally offline

Current state:
    Running
    Starting
    Loading model
    Healthy
    Failed
    Intentionally off
```

Changing desired state is an audited admin mutation.

## B4. Bounded recovery

Never create an infinite restart loop.

Suggested default:

```text
VM start attempts:       2
service restart attempts: 2
cooldown:                configurable
then:
    mark FAILED
    remove from routing
    alert operator
```

---

# 5. Phase C — Make the model registry the routing source of truth

The user-facing abstraction is the **model**, not the physical server.

A client API key should be able to use any actively hosted model by supplying the standard OpenAI `model` field.

## C1. Logical model registry

Create or normalize a registry containing at least:

```text
logical_model_name
display_name
engine
deployment_id
backend_url
host
VM
health
desired_state
routable
context_limit
concurrency_limit
capabilities
last_verified
```

Example:

```text
logical model:
    qwen3.6-35b-a3b

deployment:
    MIAM-00111 / VM103

backend:
    http://10.0.20.161:8000/v1
```

A model may later move to another host without forcing client key changes.

## C2. Routability gate

A model appears routable only when:

```text
VM desired state = RUNNING
VM current state = running
model service healthy
/v1/models succeeds
health completion succeeds
deployment not drained
deployment not in maintenance
```

## C3. `GET /v1/models`

For a normal LLM Manager agent key:

```text
GET /v1/models
```

must return the models currently available to that key.

Default policy for normal internal agent keys:

```text
allow all actively hosted/routable models
```

Do not invent a default model.

If a model is offline or intentionally powered down, either:

```text
omit it from /v1/models
```

or clearly mark it unavailable if the compatibility layer supports doing so without violating client expectations.

Prefer omission for standard OpenAI-compatible behavior.

---

# 6. Phase D — Standard OpenAI-compatible API behavior

The LLM Manager should behave like one stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint.

Clients should use:

```text
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
```

and send:

```json
{
  "model": "qwen3.6-35b-a3b",
  "messages": [...]
}
```

## D1. No default model

Do not assign a default model to an API key.

If a request omits `model`:

```text
return a normal explicit request validation error
```

Do not silently pick a model.

## D2. No deployment pinning

Do not add custom client-visible semantics such as:

```text
pin this API key to MIAM-00111
pin this key to deployment 123
```

unless a future OpenAI-compatible mechanism naturally supports it.

Routing implementation details remain server-side.

## D3. Required compatibility endpoints

Maintain at minimum:

```text
GET  /v1/models
POST /v1/chat/completions
```

Where already supported, preserve:

```text
POST /v1/responses
POST /v1/embeddings
```

but do not delay the core work to add unused endpoints.

## D4. Exact model routing

When a request specifies:

```text
model = X
```

the manager must:

```text
1. resolve X in the logical model registry
2. confirm an eligible deployment exists
3. route to that deployment
4. record which physical deployment handled it
5. return an explicit model-unavailable error if X is not currently hosted
```

Do not substitute a different model merely because it is healthy.

---

# 7. Phase E — API keys become generic agent credentials

API keys authenticate the agent.

They do **not** encode a server endpoint and do **not** assign a default model.

## E1. Default API-key behavior

A normal internal AI-agent key should permit:

```text
all actively hosted local models
```

selected through the standard request `model` field.

Keep the ability to add restrictions later, but do not make model whitelisting mandatory for normal agent deployment.

## E2. Preserve one key per agent

Each semi-persistent agent should receive its own key.

Store metadata such as:

```text
key_id
alias
project_id
agent_instance_id
agent_name
harness
created_at
last_used_at
enabled
revoked_at
monthly budget
request/token usage
```

Only show the raw key once at creation/rotation.

## E3. API-key administration UI

For each key display:

```text
alias
agent/project association
status
created
last used
requests
tokens
provider spend
estimated local compute cost
monthly cap if configured
```

Actions:

```text
block
unblock
rotate
revoke
```

Do not show a misleading "endpoint" field.

Instead show:

```text
API base:
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1

Model access:
All currently hosted models
```

---

# 8. Phase F — Make model hosting easy from the web UI

The UI should let an operator easily change what each machine is hosting.

## F1. Keep the existing Edit unit/model workflow

Each deployment card should retain:

```text
Edit unit / model
Restart model
Logs
```

and the already-built hosting-command preset/history system.

## F2. Improve the Edit unit/model modal

Show:

```text
Current active configuration
Saved hosting presets
Preset name
Engine
Model/repo
Served model name
Context limit
TP
DP
EP
quantization
KV-cache configuration
memory utilization
command/systemd unit preview
last used
use count
```

Actions:

```text
Load preset
Rename
Delete
Drag reorder
Save configuration
```

## F3. Save does not restart

The workflow must remain:

```text
Edit
-> Save
-> validate
-> show "saved; not yet applied"
```

Then the operator separately clicks:

```text
Restart model
```

The UI must show a confirmation such as:

```text
Restart qwen3.6-35b-a3b on MIAM-00111 / VM103 now?
Active requests may be interrupted.
```

Only after confirmation:

```text
drain if possible
restart service
wait for health
run verification completion
restore routability
```

## F4. Hosting a different model

When changing the served model:

```text
1. save new preset/config
2. validate paths/arguments
3. preserve old preset
4. require explicit restart
5. detect new model from /v1/models
6. update logical model registry
7. mark previous model unavailable unless another healthy deployment serves it
8. refresh LiteLLM routing
9. verify through the public LLM Manager endpoint
```

No direct manual LiteLLM YAML editing should be required for routine model changes after this phase.

---

# 9. Phase G — Initial production topology

Do not attempt multi-node serving in this plan.

## G1. Initial primary model

Keep:

```text
MIAM-00111 / VM103
QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
served name: qwen3.6-35b-a3b
```

as the first primary production deployment.

Verify through the manager:

```text
GET /v1/models
POST /v1/chat/completions
```

using a normal per-agent key.

## G2. Other hosts

MIAM-00112, MIAM-00143, MIAM-00144, and MIAM-00149 may host different models independently.

The manager should not assume that all GPU servers host the same model.

Example future state:

```text
MIAM-00111 -> qwen3.6-35b-a3b
MIAM-00112 -> another smaller/fast model
MIAM-00143 -> another specialist model
MIAM-00144 -> another model
MIAM-00149 -> startupteams/llamacpp
```

All should appear through the same `/v1/models` endpoint.

---

# 10. Phase H — Correct the cost model

The current interface must stop presenting `$0.0000` as though local inference has no cost.

There are multiple different concepts and they must be separated.

## H1. Cost categories

Display at least:

### Provider spend

```text
Actual provider spend
```

For local models:

```text
$0 provider spend
```

is legitimate.

But this is **not** total inference cost.

### Direct compute energy

Primary local metric.

Estimate direct compute power from:

```text
measured GPU watts
+
CPU/platform/RAM overhead estimate
```

Use real GPU telemetry whenever possible.

### Direct compute electricity cost

Primary local cost metric:

```text
direct_compute_kWh * current_electricity_rate
```

### Facility operational energy/cost

Secondary metric:

```text
Emporia whole-compute circuits
cooling
other facility loads
```

Keep cooling separate from the main direct-compute metric.

## H2. Required labels

Never show a calculated value without explaining what it is.

Use labels such as:

```text
Provider spend: ACTUAL
GPU energy: MEASURED
Host overhead: ESTIMATED
Direct compute cost: ESTIMATED
Facility energy: MEASURED (Emporia)
Cooling allocation: MEASURED/ESTIMATED as appropriate
```

## H3. Never fabricate zero

If telemetry is absent:

```text
—
Insufficient telemetry
Coverage incomplete
```

Do not substitute:

```text
$0.0000
```

unless the quantity being displayed is truly known to be zero.

## H4. Telemetry coverage

Every time-based cost/energy total must include telemetry coverage.

Example:

```text
Last 24h direct compute:
5.82 kWh
$0.92 estimated
telemetry coverage: 99.3%
```

If only 60 minutes of samples exist, do not label that result "24h".

## H5. Sanity test the energy integrator

Implement automated tests such as:

```text
100 watts for exactly 1 hour
= 0.100 kWh
```

and:

```text
1000 watts for exactly 1 hour
= 1.000 kWh
```

Fail deployment if the integrator does not pass.

## H6. Rate model

Keep the configured Marion/Alliant seasonal rate components versioned.

Operational planning totals currently include:

```text
winter base energy rate
summer base energy rate
year-round per-kWh fee/rider component
```

Do not collapse the components into an unexplained constant.

## H7. Request-level local cost

When enough telemetry exists, estimate per-request energy cost.

Store:

```text
request_id
model
deployment
start/end
input tokens
output tokens
TTFT
latency
average/attributed watts
estimated Wh
estimated $
confidence
```

If multiple concurrent requests overlap, explicitly use an allocation method and mark the result as estimated.

Do not imply request-level energy is utility-meter exact.

---

# 11. Phase I — Redesign Spend & Efficiency UI

The existing table should be revised so `$0.0000` cannot be misread.

Recommended columns:

```text
Model
Requests
Tokens
Provider spend
Direct compute energy
Estimated direct compute cost
Avg TTFT
Output tok/s
Cost / 1M tokens
Energy / 1M tokens
Telemetry coverage
```

For local models:

```text
Provider spend = $0.00 actual
Estimated direct compute cost = non-zero when telemetry exists
```

For OpenRouter:

```text
Provider spend = provider-reported actual
Direct local compute = n/a
```

Add help text:

```text
Provider spend and local compute cost are separate metrics.
Local model provider spend may be $0 while electricity cost is non-zero.
```

---

# 12. Phase J — Foundation for Project Implementation Manager / AI Agent Deployment

Do not build the Project Implementation Manager in this phase.

However, the LLM Manager API must be designed so the future system can use it cleanly.

Future architecture:

```text
Project Implementation Manager
        |
        v
AI Agent Deployment Service
        |
        +-- instantiate VM/LXC/container/process
        +-- select harness
        +-- create semi-persistent agent identity
        +-- request LLM Manager API key
        +-- inject:
              OPENAI_BASE_URL
              OPENAI_API_KEY
        |
        v
Agent chooses model per normal OpenAI request
```

## J1. Add machine-to-machine key lifecycle API

Provide authenticated administrative endpoints suitable for the future deployment service:

```text
create agent key
rotate agent key
revoke agent key
disable/enable agent key
query key metadata
query key usage
```

Use an LLDAP/service identity or other strong internal service authentication.

Do not expose the LiteLLM master key to the future deployment service.

## J2. Idempotent provisioning

The future service should be able to send an external identity such as:

```text
project_id
agent_instance_id
```

If provisioning is retried, the LLM Manager should not create duplicate agents/keys accidentally.

Use an idempotency key or stable external ID.

## J3. Return only what the deployment service needs

On creation return:

```text
agent/key ID
raw API key once
OpenAI base URL
metadata
```

Do **not** return:

```text
physical backend IP
provider master credentials
server SSH details
```

## J4. Agent model discovery

The deployed agent can call:

```text
GET /v1/models
```

with its own API key.

It then selects the model using the standard OpenAI request body.

This keeps the future deployment service decoupled from physical inference topology.

---

# 13. Phase K — Health-aware model availability

A model must disappear from the usable routing pool if its deployment is unhealthy.

## K1. Health loop

For each desired-online managed deployment collect:

```text
VM status
guest reachability
service status
/v1/models
small completion
GPU health where applicable
```

## K2. Failure transition

If unhealthy:

```text
remove from routing first
attempt bounded recovery
retest
restore only when healthy
```
